(109)

If we integrate both sides over the sphere, the exact piece will drop out—Stokes’ theorem forces its integral to vanish. We find, however, that

\Delta\oint_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathchoice{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}=4\pi C.

(110)

This can also be written in terms of
\mathcal{H}
. We find

\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\oint_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathchoice{H_{{{u}{A}{B}}}}{H_{{{u}{A}{B}}}}{H_{{{u}{A}{B}}}}{H_{{{u}{A}{B}}}}\differential{u}=4\pi C.

(111)

Use Eq. (
92a
) to rewrite the integral in terms of
\phi
. At leading order, we find

\displaystyle=4\pi C,

(112a)

(112b)

where
\phi^{(1)}
is the

coefficient for

in a large-

measures the change in

(113)

Notice coexact functions correspond to functions whose average over the sphere vanishes.

(117b)

Since we only consider the exterior derivative of
C
, we have to take into account the gauge-for-gauge symmetry in
C
. Fixing Lorenz gauge with
\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}=0
, we find

\Delta\mathchoice{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{u}{r}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{u}{r}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{u}{r}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{u}{r}}}}=(\mathcal{D}^{2}-1)\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}.

(118)

This expression can now be inverted, and thus
\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}
is uniquely defined in terms of the change in the “Coulombic field,”
\Delta\mathchoice{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{u}{r}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{u}{r}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{u}{r}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyl . Notice the solution is only unique once we have fixed
\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}=0
.

VI
Asymptotic Symmetries

We consider the two-form theory in Lorenz gauge. Recall, as discussed in Sec.
III.1
, of the presence of reducible gauge symmetries.

Generically, the theory is such that the field-strenght
\mathcal{H}
is given by a two-form potential

\mathcal{H}=\differential\mathcal{B}.

(119)

In Lorenz gauge, this two-form potential is required to obey

\differential{\star}\mathcal{B}=0.

(120)

The residual gauge symmetries are the one-forms obeying

\differential{\star}\differential\mathcal{A}=0,

(121)

which are taken to be already gauge-fixed in Lorenz gauge

\differential{\star}\mathcal{A}=0.

(122)

Because the original theory has reducible symmetries, these residual gauge symmetries also have residual gauge-for-gauge symmetry, which is parameterized by scalar functions obeying

\differential{\star}\differential\lambda=0.

(123)

As in the electromagnetic case, there are subtleties requiring the use of logarithms in the large-
r
expansions for the gauge parameters—see Refs.
58
;
36
;
80
. This is why we will use the expansion in Eq. (
104
).

Let us first write the Lorenz gauge condition. It will be convenient to define a scalar field
\mathcal{L}
by

\mathcal{L}=\differential{\star}\mathcal{A}.

(124)

In this way, we will be able to see how the Lorenz gauge can be used to simplify the wave equation for
\mathcal{A}
later on. Notice Lorenz gauge means
\mathcal{L}=\differential{\star}\mathcal{A}=0
. In Bondi coordinates, we have

\mathcal{L}=\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partialderivative{r}\quantity(r^2 \tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_u})-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partialderivative{r}\quantity(r^2 \tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_r})-\frac{\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}}{r^{2}}=0.

(125)

Meanwhile, the wave equation
\differential{\star}\differential\mathcal{A}=0
is given by

\displaystyle\frac{2}{r}\partialderivative{r}\quantity(r\tensor{\partial}{_u}\tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_u})-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partialderivative{r}\quantity(r^2\tensor{\partial}{_r}\tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_u})-\frac{\mathcal{D}^{2}\mathchoice{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}}{r^{2}}-\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\mathcal{L}=0,

(126a)

\displaystyle \tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_u}}{r} - \frac{2 \tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_r}}{r})-\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}\mathcal{L}=0.

(126c)

Considering the analysis by
80
, we define a new scalar

\mathcal{W}=\quantity[\frac{2}{r}\pdv{r}\qty(r\tensor{\partial}{_u}\tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_r}) - \frac{1}{r^2}\pdv{r}\qty(r^2\tensor{\partial}{_r}\tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_r}) - \frac{(\mathcal{D}^2-2)\tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_r}}{r^2} - \frac{2\tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_u}}{r^2} +\frac{2 \tensor{\mathcal{D}}{^B}\tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_B}}{r^3} - \tensor{\partial}{_r}\mathcal{L}]-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partialderivative{r}\quantity(r^2\mathcal{L}).

(127)

The term in brackets is the
r
-component of the wave equations. If we assume the wave equations to hold, then

\mathcal{W}\approx-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partialderivative{r}\quantity(r^2\mathcal{L}),

(128)

where “
\approx
” stands for “equality on-shell.” This which means that, once the wave equation is imposed,
\mathcal{W}
vanishes if, and only if,

\partialderivative{r}\quantity(r^2\mathcal{L})=0.

(129)

This means
\mathcal{W}
vanishes if, and only if,

\mathcal{L}=\frac{L^{(2)}}{r^{2}}.

(130)

Hence, once the equations of motion are imposed,
\mathcal{L}
vanishes if, and only if,
\mathcal{W}
and
L^{(2)}
vanish. This is convenient because

\mathcal{W}=\frac{1}{r^{4}}\partialderivative{r}\quantity(r^4\tensor{\partial}{_r}\tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_r})-\frac{(\mathcal{D}^{2}-2)\mathchoice{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}}{r^{2}}-\frac{2\mathchoice{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}}{r^{2}}-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\partialderivative{r}\quantity(r^3 \tensor{\partial}{_r}\tensor{\mathcal{A}}{_u})+\frac{2\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{r}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\ (131)

which has no
u
-derivatives. This will be convenient in what follows.

In terms of the expansion (
104
), and writing

\mathcal{L}\sim\sum_{k=0}\frac{L^{(k)}}{r^{k}}+\sum_{k=1}\frac{\hat{L}^{(k)}\log{r}}{r^{k}},

(132)

and

\mathcal{W}\sim\sum_{k=0}\frac{W^{(k)}}{r^{k}}+\sum_{k=1}\frac{\hat{W}^{(k)}\log{r}}{r^{k}},

(133)

we find that

\displaystyle (134a)

\displaystyle\hat{L}^{(n)}=\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}-(n-3)\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(n-1)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n-1)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n-1)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n-1)}}}_{{{u}}}}+(n-3)\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(n-1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n-1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n-1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n-1)}}}_{{{r}}}}-\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scri (134b)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle W^{(n+2)}=-2(n-1)\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{A^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}{A^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}{A^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}{A^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}+2\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}-(2n-3)\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}+4(n-1)\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}} (135a)

\displaystyle\hat{W}^{(n+2)}=-2(n-1)\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{B}}}}-[\mathcal{D}^{2}-(n-1)(n-2)]\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{r}}}}-2(n-1)^{2}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(n)}}}_{{{u}}}}.

(135b)

To impose Lorenz gauge when solving the wave equation, we merely need to impose
L^{(2)}=0
and
W^{(n)}=0
for all
n
. Notice, though, that
W^{(n)}
does not mix different orders in
r
.

At last, the wave equation becomes

L^{(2)}=0
. This condition is

Apart from this condition, we can solve the system by working order-by-order with the wave equations and
\mathcal{W}
.

Let us write the equations involving the leading-order data for
\mathcal{A}
. The gauge condition
W^{(2)}=0
imposes

2\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{B}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{B}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{B}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{B}}}}-[\mathcal{D}^{2}-2]\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}-2\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}=0.

(138)

Meanwhile, the wave equations imply (
n=0
in Lorenz gauge)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}=0,

(139a)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}=0,

(139b)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}=0.

(139c)

We see that the leading components of
\mathcal{A}
are required to be time-independent.

Can higher-order terms further constrain these initial data? For
n=1
, the gauge condition
\mathcal{W}=0
implies

\displaystyle W^{(3)}=2\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{B}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{B}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{B}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{B}}}}+\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}-\mathcal{D}^{2}\mathchoice{A^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}=0,

(140a)

\displaystyle\hat{W}^{(3)}=-\mathcal{D}^{2}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}=0.

(140b)

The equation
\mathcal{D}^{2}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}=0
means
\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}
is constant on the sphere. However, the imposition
W^{(3)}=0
forces
\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}
to have vanishing average on the sphere. Thus,
\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}=0
. In the wave equations we have, at
n=1
and Lorenz gauge,

\displaystyle 2\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{u}}}}-\mathcal{D}^{2}\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}=0,

(141a)

\displaystyle 2\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}-[\mathcal{D}^{2}-2]\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}-2\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}+2\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{B}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{B}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{B}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{B}}}}=0,

(141b)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{A}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{A}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{A}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{A}}}}-[\mathcal{D}^{2}-1]\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}-2\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}+2\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}=0.

(141c)

We already know
\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}=0
, and indeed Eq. (
138
) means the expression for
\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{r}}}}
vanishes, as it should. The remaining equations are differential equations setting the time-evolution of
\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{u}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{u}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{A}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{A}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{A}}}}{\hat{A}^{{{(1)}}}_{{{A}}}}
in terms of previously known data. We see that no new conditions for the leading data on
\mathcal{A}
appear. This would not be the case had we not added the logarithmic series in Eq. (
104
), because then Eq. (
141
) would constrain
\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}
,
\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}
, and
\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}
even more.

As we progress for larger values of
n
, the subleading data is determined by the leading data, as in the electromagnetic case.

We still have residual gauge-to-gauge freedom in
\mathcal{A}
. By doing a shift

\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}+\differential\lambda,

(142)

with
\differential{\star}\differential\lambda=0
, we can still preserve Lorenz gauge. Pick

\lambda\sim r\lambda^{(-1)}+\log{r}\hat{\lambda}^{(0)}+\lambda^{(0)}+o(1),

(143)

which is more general than Eq. (
78
) because we are admitting larger falloffs for
\mathcal{A}
in comparison to what we did in electrodynamics. Then, the first few orders of the wave equation for
\lambda
yield

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\lambda^{(-1)}=0,

(144a)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\lambda^{(0)}=\frac{1}{2}\quantity[\mathcal{D}^2 + 6]\lambda^{(-1)},

(144b)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{u}}}}\hat{\lambda}^{(0)}=0,

(144c)

and so on. In practice, we can obtain residual gauge-for-gauge transformations with the behavior

\lambda=r\varepsilon(\mathchoice{x^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}})+\log{r}\hat{\varepsilon}(\mathchoice{x^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}})+\order{1}.

(145)

By setting
\varepsilon=-\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}
, we can eliminate
\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{r}}}}
from the leading order data. Equation (
138
) then determines
\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{u}}}}
from
\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}
, which we are free to specify up to an exact form (which is the gauge transformation
\lambda
).

Hence, we notice the existence of nontrivial residual gauge transformations that do not vanish at infinity. These are given by

\mathchoice{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\differential{\tensor{x}{^\mu}}=r\mathchoice{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}{A^{{{(0)}}}_{{{A}}}}\differential{\tensor{x}{^A}}+o(r).

(146)

In terms of
\mathcal{B}
, we see these large gauge transformations act on
\mathchoice{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}
according to

\mathchoice{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}\to\mathchoice{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scr (147)

All other components of
\mathcal{B}
are shifted in orders smaller than
r
.

ce{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}+C\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice (148)

where
\mathchoice{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{A}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}
is a large gauge transformation for the two-form description of acoustics,
C
is a constant, and
\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
is the volume form on the unit sphere. Hence, the memory tensor can be written a large gauge transformation, except in the particular case of monopole-induced memory.

VII
Soft Theorem

The asymptotic symmetries we obtained are the same ones analyzed by
17
;
35
. Their goal was to understand, in terms of a two-form, the asymptotic symmetries that would originate a scalar soft theorem
16
. Hence, the connection between the asymptotic symmetries discussed above and a scalar soft theorem is already known. In particular,
17
highlight how the
C
-term in our memory tensor receives a different treatment in the two-form description. It is associated to a symmetry that cannot be written as a large gauge transformation in general, and has no interpretation in the two-form theory. Indeed, our interpretation above of this term was as a permanent shift in the monopole of the scalar field
\phi
.

Our task is then to find a link between this soft theorem and the memory effect. A key remark is that soft theorems are typically connected to memory effects via a Braginsky–Thorne–like formula
15
;
83
. See, for example, Sec. 6.6 in Ref.
27
. This is very natural for theories coupled to charged particles, such as gravitation and electrodynamics. For sound, however, the sources we consider are merely “image sources,” not physical particles in a quantum or classical sense.

If we overlook this fact, we can consider a set of pointlike sources and study the memory effect associated to them. For one pointlike source following a trajectory
\mathbf{z}(t)
, we pick

q(t,\mathbf{r})=\frac{Q}{\sqrt{1-v^{2}/c^{2}}}\delta^{(3)}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{z}(t)),

(149)

where
v
is the norm of the velocity of the particle and
Q
is a constant. Notice we effectively introduced a “sonic” Lorentz factor in the expression for the density of a pointlike charge. In relativity, this is the combination that would be Lorentz invariant for a scalar charge. It originates from

\displaystyle q(x)

\displaystyle=Q\int\delta^{(4)}(x-z(\tau))\differential{\tau},

(150a)

\displaystyle=Q\int\delta(t-\mathchoice{z^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{z^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{z^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{z^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(\tau))\delta^{(3)}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{z}(\tau))\differential{\tau},

(150b)

\displaystyle=Q\derivative{\tau}{t}\delta^{(3)}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{z}(t)).

(150c)

If we choose to consider such a source, then the wave equation becomes

-\frac{1}{c^{2}}\partialderivative[2]{\phi}{t}+\laplacian\phi=\frac{Q}{\sqrt{1-v^{2}/c^{2}}}\delta^{(3)}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{z}(t)),

(151)

and the retarded solution is given by

\phi(t,\mathbf{r})=-\frac{Qc}{4\pi\sqrt{c^{2}-v^{2}}}\frac{c}{c-\hat{\mathbf{n}}\dotproduct\mathbf{v}}\frac{1}{\norm{\vb{r} - \vb{z}(t_{\text{ret}})}},

(152)

where

\hat{\mathbf{n}}=\frac{\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{z}(t_{\text{ret}})}{\norm{\vb{r} - \vb{z}(t_{\text{ret}})}},

(153)

t_{\text{ret}}
is defined by

c(t-t_{\text{ret}})=\norm{\vb{r} - \vb{z}(t_{\text{ret}})},

(154)

and the velocities are always evaluated at retarded time. This construction may seem arbitrary, but remember the monopole
Q
is not a fundamental property of the sources. The sources are not particles in any sense. Rather, they are a convenient way of phrasing boundary conditions through the method of images. In introducing a Lorentz-like factor, we are effectively redefining the meaning of “monopole” in a way that will be convenient later on. For an inertial particle, this just means multiplying
Q
by a constant.

If we consider a scattering process with a number of incoming sources, and a number of outgoing sources, then the memory in
\phi
is

\Delta\phi(\mathbf{r})=-\frac{c^{2}}{4\pi r}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\\
\text{incoming}\end{subarray}}\frac{Q_{i}}{\sqrt{c^{2}-v_{i}^{2}}(c-\hat{\mathbf{r}}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{i})}\right.\\
\left.{}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\\
\text{outgoing}\end{subarray}}\frac{Q_{j}}{\sqrt{c^{2}-v_{j}^{2}}(c-\hat{\mathbf{r}}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{j})}\right].

(155)

Because the sources are not fundamental, this expression is not as general and far-reaching as the original Braginsky–Thorne formula
15
. Nevertheless, it still bears physical meaning on a finely-tuned experiment. For instance, it can describe the memory in the acoustic field given a scattering process in which there is a number of incoming sources with constant monopole (i.e., constant volume time-derivative) and a number of similar outgoing sources. As the simplest possible example, it recovers the case of a pulsating sphere that starts at rest, has a complicated dynamics at intermediate times, and ends at a constant volume-change-rate for the experiment’s purposes.

The denominators in Eq. (
155
) can be rewritten as

\sqrt{c^{2}-v^{2}}(c-\hat{\mathbf{r}}\cdot\mathbf{v})=-\mathchoice{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}},

(156)

where
\mathchoice{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
is akin to a four-velocity, with components

\mathchoice{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\sqrt{1-\frac{v^{2}}{c^{2}}}(c,\mathbf{v}),

while

(158)

Above, we consired the coordinate basis to be
(ct,x,y,z)
, for simplicity in writing the components.

Given Eq. (
156
), one can see that the kinematical combinations that occur in the soft theorems considered by
16
are precisely of the form of Eq. (
155
). Hence, with a suitable choice of coupling constants in the quantum field theory, the Braginsky–Thorne formula for the acoustic memory can be reproduced from the soft theorem. We omit the detailed calculations, since they are completely analogous to the standard results for gravity, for example—see, e.g., Sec. 6.6 in Ref.
27
.

VIII
Discussion

We have discussed an infrared triangle for linear perturbations in inviscid, irrotational, barotropic fluid flow. We have shown how it can present a memory effect, and how a dual formulation in terms of a two-form allows us to understand this memory effect in terms of asymptotic symmetries. These asymptotic symmetries, in turn, can be associated to soft scalar theorems, which can also be used to reproduce the memory effect. Two key consequences of this analysis are the following:

1.

Asymptotic symmetries and the infrared triangle are not restricted to the realm of high-energy physics, and can also be realized in condensed matter systems.

2.

In addition to the experimental prospects to detect gravitational wave memory
41
and electromagnetic memory
9
, it is also possible to expect measurements of acoustic memory in a laboratory
26
. Furthermore, the observation of such effects would also admit an interpretation in terms of asymptotic symmetries.

Several comments are in order at this point. First and foremost, we discuss why a memory effect for sound is expected. The key remark is that Eqs. (
15
), (
18
), and (
19
) are not specific to sound or any theory in particular. They are all derived considering a (scalar) wave equation using the retarded Green’s function, and then performing a large-
r
expansion. One immediately sees from Eq. (
19
) that if
Q
asymptotes to different constants at early and late retarded times, then there will be a memory effect in the sense that
\Delta\phi
will be nonzero at order
1/r
. This is not a property of any specific theory—be it general relativity, Yang–Mills, electrodynamics, acoustics, or else—but rather a very general property of the wave equation in four dimensions. There are no nonlinear effects involved and this memory shift does not depend on a probe in any way. It is the response of the field to a permanent change in the source.

Relativistic theories are often coupled to conserved sources. For example, electric charge is conserved, and so is energy-momentum. This leads to particularly well-behaved memory effects, which can naturally arise in scattering. Since electric charges or masses cannot be created nor destroyed, simply changing their kinematic configurations can be enough to create memory. For instance, the original gravitational-wave displacement memory effect was conceived by considering the scattering of a cluster of stars and other celestial objects
100
;
14
;
15
. Acoustics, on the other hand, is not coupled to a conserved source. A sphere with constant radius, for instance, has a vanishing monopole moment, and does not produce any kind of sound. This is a sense in which memory in effective theories can be unusual, because physical realizations may need to handle the sources with more care than in their fundamental counterparts.

Once it is clear that a memory effect for sound can be obtained—which, as stated, is a direct consequence of the wave equation—one can inquire about connections to the other corners of an infrared triangle. Within the approximations we worked on, acoustic perturbations are mathematically identical to a massless Klein–Gordon field in a relativistic scenario. This allowed to explore a duality between the Klein–Gordon field and a two-form theory, which had already been investigated before in the context of asymptotic symmetries
17
;
35
, but—to our knowledge—no connections to a memory effect had been investigated so far.

Given the previous work by
17
;
35
, it is expected that scalar fields admit asymptotic symmetries in some sense, and thus that this could be carried over to sound. Nevertheless, it is nontrivial a priori that these symmetries can still describe the long-distance behavior predicted by the Euler equations. This nontriviality is due to how we define “memory.” For a relativistic scalar field, the “natural” memory effect would be a momentum kick to a probe, as discussed by
90
, for example. We, however, defined the “acoustic memory effect” to be a permanent shift in the Lagrangian displacement of a fluid particle. This is physically very different from the momentum kick. Yet, both effects can be tracked down to a permanent shift in the scalar field, which in turn could be expected to be explained in terms of dual asymptotic symmetries.

There is one notable exception to the asymptotic symmetry interpretation. In Sec.
V
we highlighted how the memory tensor is not an exact form on the sphere, and in Sec.
VI
we only related the exact part of the memory tensor to asymptotic symmetries. The nonexact part was instead associated, in Sec.
V
, to a permanent shift in the monopole term of
\phi
. The same sort of difficulty was identified by
17
, albeit in that case they were concerned with interpreting the surface charges of a scalar theory in terms of dual asymptotic symmetries. In this sense, our result reproduces the difficulty they found.

The existence of memory that cannot be related to asymptotic symmetries is not a special property of sound. In fact, as shown by
80
, general relativity also has memory effects that cannot be written in terms of a diffeomorphism. In the case of gravity, this is intimately related to how the memory tensor is decomposed in terms of scalars, vectors, and tensors on the sphere
80
. Our result is astonishingly similar. Equation (
101
) gives the Hodge decomposition
37
;
65
of the memory tensor (see the Appendix). It uniquely writes the memory two-form in terms of an exact piece and a harmonic piece. The exact piece if fundamentally a one-form, while the harmonic piece is fundamentally a two-form (or scalar, through Hodge duality). In the acoustic theory, “exact memory” is a large gauge transformation, but “harmonic memory” is not associated to a large gauge transformation.

Last, but not least, we discuss the soft theorems. Within acoustics, there is once again the issue that fundamental sources are not available, and thus all sources are “image sources.” This introduces a difficulty in formulating what would be a feasible quantum theory in which the soft theorems should capture the memory effect. In this work, we limited ourselves to a proof of principle. If the image sources are chosen so that they have asymptotically constant monopoles, it is possible to construct a quantum field theory whose soft theorems would reproduce the memory effect. This should not be seen as a deep statement about acoustics, but rather as an application of the fact that scattering amplitudes can be used as a computational technique even in classical physics
52
;
25
;
51
;
64
. The connection between these soft theorems and the asymptotic symmetries we identified had already been made by
16
;
17
;
35
.

There are many development directions in sight. First and foremost, we comment on the approximations we did when describing fluid flow. Our calculations mostly concerned linear perturbations in inviscid, irrotational, and barotropic fluid flows. It would be interesting to improve on all of these approximations. The key points in which the irrotational and barotropic assumptions enter the calculations are in obtaining the equations of motion for the three-form
\mathcal{H}
, in Sec.
III
. To obtain the expression
\differential{\star}\mathcal{H}=0
, we chose to ignore these effects. It is possible that further details and more general flows could be incorporated by modifying the equations of motion to allow sources for
\mathcal{H}
. Indeed, the two-form formalism for hydrodynamics discussed by
66
;
86
;
62
allows the fluid itself to play the role of a source to introduce both vorticity and nonlinearity. Finally, viscous adaptations would also require further changes to the equations of motion.

Finally, in this work we focused on a linear memory effect, and considered a simple “toy soft theorem” as a proof of principle that an infrared triangle is available in linear acoustics. Natural development directions would be to generalize our analysis to consider the nonlinear memory effect predicted by
26
, and/or the soft phonon theorems derived by
21
.

Part of the calculations in this work were carried out with the aid of
Mathematica 14.0

99
, and in particular the
OGRe
package
81
and the
xAct
suite
60
;
61
.

and

(161b)

Because the index structure of a
p
-form is trivial, we may often omit the indices altogether. For instance, we may simply write
\omega
instead of
\mathchoice{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.30296pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.30296pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.85103pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.111pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.30296pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.30296pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.85103pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.111pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.30296pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.30296pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.85103pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.111pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\mak
