.

The space of
p
-form fields is denoted
\Omega^{p}(M)
.
\Omega^{0}(M)
is understood as
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
, the space of smooth functions on the manifold. Notice that, for an
n
-dimensional manifold
M
,
\Omega^{n+1}(M)
is trivial, because it is impossible to write a nonvanishing antisymmetric tensor field with
n+1
indices. Similarly, all elements of
\Omega^{n}(M)
can be written as a function multiplying a standard basis element
\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1909pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.45087pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\ldots}{a_{n}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1909pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.45087pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\ldots}{a_{n}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1909pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.45087pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\ldots}{a_{n}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1909pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.45087pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\ldots}{a_{n}}}}}
.

There are some natural operations that can be defined among
p
-forms. For example, we can define the wedge product. This takes the tensor product of two forms and antisymmetrizes the result to ensure it is also a differential form. In detail,

(162)

The numerical coefficient is a matter of convention.

Even in the absence of a metric or any other extra structure, it is possible to introduce a derivative operator among differential forms. This is known as the exterior derivative
\differential
. For each
p
, we define
\differential\colon\Omega^{p}(M)\to\Omega^{p+1}(M)
by

\mathchoice{\quantity(\dd{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.32326pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.58322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p+1}}}}}{\quantity(\dd{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.32326pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.58322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p+1}}}}}{\quantity(\dd{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.32326pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.58322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p+1}}}}}{\quantity(\dd{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$ (163)

where
\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}}
is any (torsionless) differential operator on the manifold. The antisymmetrization cancels the effect of any connection coefficients, and thus the choice of differential operator does not affect the result. In particular, one can merely use partial derivatives.

Notice that the antisymmetrization in the definition of the exterior derivative ensures it is nilpotent. More specifically, notice that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{(\differential[2]{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.32326pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.58322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p+2}}}}}{(\differential[2]{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.32326pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.58322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p+2}}}}}{(\differential[2]{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56407pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.32326pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.58322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p+2}}}}}{(\differential[2]{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makeb (164a)

\displaystyle=(p+2)(p+1)\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{[}{a_{1}}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{[}{a_{1}}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{[}{a_{1}}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{[}{a_{1}}}}}\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{2}}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{2}}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\mak (164b)

\displaystyle=0,

(164c)

because partial derivatives in any particular coordinate system always commute. Hence
\differential[]=0
.

A.1
De Rham cohomology

Since
\differential[]=0
, it is true that

\differential\differential\omega=0

(165)

for any
p
-form
\omega
. Suppose, however, that
\mu
is a
(p+1)
-form with

\differential\mu=0.

(166)

How can we know if there is a
p
-form
\omega
such that
\mu=\differential\omega
?

This problem is addressed by de Rham cohomology—see, e.g., Refs.
37
;
40
;
55
;
65
;
91
for various introductions. Let us first notice that the exterior derivative allows us to write a sequence of maps according to

0\rightarrow\Omega^{0}(M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\differential_{0}}}{{\rightarrow}}\Omega^{1}(M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\differential_{1}}}{{\rightarrow}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\differential_{n-1}}}{{\rightarrow}}\Omega^{n}(M)\rightarrow 0,

(167)

where we wrote
\differential_{p}\colon\Omega^{p}(M)\to\Omega^{p+1}(M)
for clarity of which map is which. Because all
(n+1)
-forms in an
n
-dimensional manifold vanish, the exterior derivative applied to
\Omega^{n}(M)
maps all
n
-forms to the zero
(n+1)
-form.

Two spaces are particularly interesting here. One of them is the kernel

Z^{p}(M)=\mathrm{Ker}\,\differential_{p}=\quantity{\omega\in\Omega^p(M) \mid\dd\omega= 0}.

(168)

The second is the image

B^{p}(M)=\mathrm{Ran}\,\differential_{p-1}=\quantity{\dd\omega\mid\omega\in\Omega^{p-1}(M)}.

(169)

Notice that
B^{p}(M)\subseteq Z^{p}(M)\subseteq\Omega^{p}(M)
. The elements of
Z^{p}(M)
are said to be closed. The elements of
B^{p}(M)
are said to be exact. We want to understand when a closed form fails to be exact. This means we want to understand what are the elements of the quotient

H^{p}(M)=Z^{p}(M)/B^{p}(M),

(170)

which is known as the
p
-th de Rham cohomology group for the manifold
M
. Although they are called groups, they are real vector spaces.

Notice that we defined the de Rham cohomology groups using the differentiable structure of the manifold. Indeed, we needed to define smooth differential forms in order to construct them. In spite of this, the so-called de Rham theorem ensures the de Rham cohomology groups are topological invariants
37
;
65
.

The simplest example is that of Euclidean space. One has
37
;
65
;
91

H^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{R},&\text{ if }k=0,\\
0,&\text{ if }k>0,\end{cases}

(171)

where “
0
” is used in the second line to denote the trivial vector space. Hence, in Euclidean space, all closed forms are exact. This is known as Poincaré’s lemma. On a manifold, one can always pick a local coordinate chart with the topology of Euclidean space. It then follows that, in this chart, all closed forms are exact. Hence, in a general manifold, all closed forms are locally exact, meaning it is always possible to write a closed form
\mu
as
\mu=\differential\omega
on a sufficiently small region.

Our main case of interest is that of a two-sphere. One then has that
37
;
65

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathbb{S}^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{R},

(172a)

\displaystyle H^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2})

\displaystyle=0,

(172b)

\displaystyle H^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{R}.

(172c)

In fact,
H^{0}(M)=\mathbb{R}
whenever
M
is connected, because the constant
0
-forms are closed, but cannot be exact, since
B^{0}(M)=0
—there are no
(-1)
-forms. The remaining groups are specific to the sphere. Notice

\mathrm{dim}\,H^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})=1,

(173)

where the dimension is meant in the sense of real vector spaces. In this sense, there is a single two-form that is closed, but not exact. This is the volume two-form on the sphere,

\epsilon=\sin\theta\differential{\theta}\wedge\differential{\varphi},

(174)

where
\theta
is the polar angle and
\varphi
is the azimuthal angle. Notice we say that “there is a single two-form that is closed, but not exact” in the sense that, if
\omega\in\Omega^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
is closed, but not exact, then it always holds that

\omega=\differential\mu+c\epsilon,

(175)

where
\mu\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
and
c
is a real number.

In the main text, we often work in the “radiation zone,” modeled as the manifold
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
. It can be shown that

H^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})=H^{p}(\mathbb{S}^{2})

(176)

for all
p
(this follows from the Poincaré lemma and the so-called Künneth formula
37
).

A.2
Hodge theory

As mentioned above, all
n
-forms in an
n
-dimensional manifold can be written as a function multiplying a “standard form”
\epsilon
. If we are working in a pseudo-Riemannian manifold, then the choice of a “standard form” is specified up to a choice of sign. More specifically, it is convenional to pick

\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1909pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.45087pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{n}}}}}=(-1)^{s}n!,

(177)

where indices are raised with the metric and
s
is the number of negative signs in the metric signature. It then follows that

{{a_{n}}{]}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.95781pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.95781pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.70372pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10289pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.7788pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1909pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.45087pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b_{n}}}}}.

(179)

Once a choice of sign for
\epsilon
has been made, it is possible to define an operator
{\star}\colon\Omega^{p}(M)\to\Omega^{n-p}(M)
known as the Hodge dual. We define

\mathchoice{({\star}\omega)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.87744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.87744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.1185pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.37846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{n-p}}}}}{({\star}\omega)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.87744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.87744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.1185pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.37846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{n-p}}}}}{({\star}\omega)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.87744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.87744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.1185pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.37846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{n-p}}}}}{({\star}\omega)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textst 4.3529pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.75208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.48198pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.48198pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.36385pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.76302pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.87744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.87744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.1185pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.37846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b_{1}}{\cdots}{b_{p}}{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{n-p}}}}},

(180)

where indices are raised with the metric. Notice that

{\star}{\star}\omega=(-1)^{s+p(n-p)}\omega.

(181)

Using the Hodge dual and the exterior derivative, we can define the codifferential
\variation\colon\Omega^{p}(M)\to\Omega^{p-1}(M)
through

\variation=(-1)^{s+1+n(p+1)}{\star}\differential{\star}=(-1)^{p}{\star}^{-1}\differential{\star}.

(182)

Notice
{\star}^{-1}
is acting on a
(n-p+1)
-form. For Lorentzian manifolds in four dimensions we get

\variation={\star}\differential{\star}.

(183)

For Riemannian manifolds in two dimensions we get

\variation=-{\star}\differential{\star}.

(184)

In the main text, we prefer to write
\differential
and
{\star}
explicitly and
never
write the codifferential explicitly. This avoids mistaking it with the variation or perturbation of various quantities. Nevertheless, understanding the codifferential can simplify some expressions. As a general rule, it holds that

\mathchoice{\quantity(\var{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.76768pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.02765pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p-1}}}}}{\quantity(\var{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.76768pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.02765pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p-1}}}}}{\quantity(\var{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.76768pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.02765pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p-1}}}}}{\quantity(\var{\omega})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displayst 9pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.76768pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.02765pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p-1}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.76768pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.02765pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p-1}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.38629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.76768pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.02765pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}{a_{1}}{\cdots}{a_{p-1}}}}},

(185)

and thus it is akin to the divergence of a differential form, while the exterior derivative is similar to the curl.

A form
\mu
with
\differential\mu=0
is closed, and a form
\mu
with
\mu=\differential\omega
is exact. Analogously, a form
\mu
with
\variation\mu=0
is coclosed, and a form
\mu
with
\mu=\variation\omega
is coexact.
\mu
being coclosed is equivalent to
{\star}\mu
being closed, and
\mu
being coexact is equivalent to
{\star}\mu
being exact.

The codifferential and the exterior derivative can be used together to define the Laplacian on forms. This is known as the Laplace–de Rham operator, or the Hodge Laplacian. It is given by

\Delta_{\text{H}}\omega=(\differential\variation+\variation\differential)\omega.

(186)

{]}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.30296pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.30296pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.85103pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.111pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{|}{b}{c}{|}{a_{3}}{\cdots}{a_{p}}{]}}}}.

(187)

As a key example, let us consider four-dimensional electrodynamics in Lorentzian signature. The equations of motion are

\displaystyle\differential\mathcal{F}=0,

(188a)

\displaystyle\differential{\star}\mathcal{F}=4\pi{\star}\,j.

(188b)

In terms of the codifferential we have

\displaystyle\differential\mathcal{F}=0,

(189a)

\displaystyle\variation\mathcal{F}=4\pi j.

(189b)

Because
\mathcal{F}
is closed, we can introduce a potential
\mathcal{A}
at least locally. This means the equations of motion become

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\differential\mathcal{A},

(190a)

\displaystyle\variation\differential\mathcal{A}=4\pi j.

(190b)

This holds for a general gauge. In Lorenz gauge, we impose
\differential{\star}\mathcal{A}=0
. In terms of the codifferential,
\variation\mathcal{A}=0
. Hence, Lorenz gauge means imposing the potential is coclosed. With this in mind, we see we can now write Maxwell’s equations as

\displaystyle\variation\differential\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle=4\pi j,

(191a)

\displaystyle(\variation\differential+\differential\variation)\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle=4\pi j,

(191b)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\text{H}}\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle=4\pi j.

(191c)

By similar arguments, we see
\variation\differential
(or
\differential{\star}\differential
, as we write in the main text) is always understood as a wave operator when acting on coclosed forms (i.e., on fields in Lorenz gauge). Notice too has scalars are always coclosed.

For us, the particular case of a two-sphere is particularly interesting. Then the Riemann tensor is considerably simplified, and we find

\displaystyle\Delta_{\text{H}}C=-\mathcal{D}^{2}C,

(192a)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\text{H}}\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}=-(\mathcal{D}^{2}-1)\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}},

(192b)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\text{H}}\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}=-\mathcal{D}^{2}\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}{B}}}},

(192c)

where
\mathcal{D}^{2}=\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}
is the connection Laplacian on the sphere (as in the main text).

In compact Riemannian manifolds (such as the sphere), a form
\omega
is said to be harmonic when
\Delta_{\text{H}}\omega=0
. This occurs if, and only if,
\omega
is closed and coclosed
65
;
37
. The Hodge decomposition theorem then ensures that—in compact Riemannian manifolds—one can write

\Omega^{p}(M)=\differential\Omega^{p-1}(M)\oplus\variation\Omega^{p+1}(M)\oplus\mathrm{Harm}^{p}(M),

(193)

where
\mathrm{Harm}^{p}(M)
is the space of harmonic
p
-forms. Hence, all
p
-forms on a compact Riemannian manifold can be uniquely decomposed into a sum of an exact form, a coexact form, and a harmonic form.

Finally, Hodge’s theorem states that—in a compact Riemannian manifold—the space of harmonic
p
-forms is isomorphic to the de Rham cohomology
H^{p}(M)

65
;
37
,

H^{p}(M)\cong\mathrm{Harm}^{p}(M).

(194)

In this sense, the harmonic forms are precisely the forms that are closed, but fail to be exact.
